「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる世界
〜中国の「強すぎる供給⼒」がもたらす構造変化〜
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

本資料の要旨
1
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
• 「チャイナショック」とは、中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える現象
• 「2.0」の特徴は、労働集約型産業での競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争力を高めていること
• 背景にあるのが中国特有の技術革新モデル。産業集積と規模の経済もあり、中国企業の競争力が格段に向上
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
• 欧州は輸出減少と輸入増加に直面。基幹産業でも中国製品の存在感が高まり、自動車産業を中心に競争が本格化
• ASEANは中国からの中間財調達が輸出産業にとって必須化。ただし、最終財を中心に域内産品との競合は強まる
• 中国製品の浸透を受け、欧州、ASEANとも貿易障壁措置を拡大。中国企業は貿易障壁を意識し、現地生産を志向

３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
• 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
• 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
• 中国が新興国においてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する「チャイナショック3.0」に発展する可能性も
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

2005年 2015年 2024年（％）
「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か――中国の「強すぎる供給力」が世界を変える現象
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える「チャイナショック2.0」が進行中
― 中国がWTO加盟を契機に生産・組立拠点として世界貿易体制に組み込まれたのが「チャイナショック1.0」
―「2.0」では、中国企業の競争力と供給力が飛躍的に向上し、先端産業分野で先進国との競合が激化
 「チャイナショック2.0」の特徴は、中国が労働集約型産業での輸出競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争
力を高めていること。中国企業は繊維・履物などの軽工業品からハイテク製品まで幅広い分野で輸出を伸ばしている
― 他方、「自立自強」政策による輸入代替の進展と内需の低迷で輸入が停滞。その結果、貿易不均衡が拡大
3
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

「チャイナショック2.0」はなぜ起きたのか――「中国式イノベーション」と製造業の生産性向上
 「チャイナショック2.0」の背景にあるのが「中国式イノベーション」。新興分野で中国の民営企業が躍進
― 「中国式イノベーション」とは、国家の戦略的主導と民間の市場志向が組み合わさった技術革新モデル（筆者定義）
― その土台にあるのが産業集積と規模の経済。こうした環境下、中国企業の技術力とコスト競争力が格段に向上
 中国の製造業は、政府の産業支援も背景に積極的な設備投資を行い、自動化と生産の効率化が急速に進展
― 産業用ロボットの新規導入台数（2025年）は35.4万台と世界（60.3万台）の過半を占め、稼働台数でも群を抜く
― 世界経済フォーラム（WEF）が認定するライトハウス（先進工場）拠点数で中国は最多。特に生産性の評価が高い

4
「中国式イノベーション」の特徴（筆者定義） 産業用ロボット稼働台数（左）とライトハウス拠点数（右）

製造業付加価値は世界の3割弱を占める規模。貿易構造が高度化しつつある様子も
 中国はすでに「世界の工場」の地位を確立。製造業付加価値（2025年）は4.8兆ドルと、世界の3割弱を占める規模
― 中国の輸出額（2025年）は3.77兆ドルと、世界の14.4％を占める。貿易黒字も1.18兆ドルと圧倒的な大きさ
― 輸出総額だけでなく、技術集約的なハイテク製品（航空、コンピューターなど）の輸出額も8,250億ドルと世界一
 加えて、近年は貿易構造が消費財（最終財）中心から中間財や資本財へと高度化しつつある様子もうかがえる
― より付加価値の高い製品の貿易特化係数（輸出入バランスから貿易品目の国際競争力を示す指標）が上昇傾向
5

軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械でも競争力向上

 中国は、軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械などでも競争力を向上させている
― HSコード別の貿易特化係数を見ると、2025年は2005年と比較して輸出競争力が大幅に向上している品目が多い
― 他方で、食品・原材料・素材の多くは輸入に頼る。「世界の工場」における需要拡大や食の多様化などが背景に
6

類
似
度
高
中国と先進国との輸出競合度が急上昇。自動車が最大の上昇要因
 中国と先進国の輸出類似度指数（ESI）は「チャイナショック2.0」で急上昇。両者の競合が強まっていることを示唆
― 日本は「1.0」と「2.0」の時期に、EUは2010年代中盤から「2.0」の時期にかけて、ESIが顕著に上昇
― 実際、EUは「2.0」の「震度」が大きく、中国製品の浸透による製造業の衰退が最も懸念されている地域
― 産業別に見ると、「2.0」では日本、EUともに自動車が最大の上昇要因になっていることが分かる
10

①チャイナショック2.0とは何か

### Page 12

「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
 「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。「自立自強」による輸入代替（国産化）の進展が背景に
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアを見ると、輸出が「1.0」の頃からほぼ一貫して上昇しているのに対し、輸入は2010
年代に入って頭打ちとなっていることが分かる
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小傾向にある

### Page 22

ASEAN5：国産品・中国産品の調達額変化の比較
ASEAN：一方、中国産品とASEAN域内産品の競合は強まる傾向
21
 素材や消費財、機械・電気機器等では、中国製品がASEAN域内産品を圧迫。最終需要で競合する分野が多い傾向
― 上記分野では中国産品の供給増と域内産品の供給減が同時に発生。中国産品が域内産品を上回って増加する例も
― 中間需要は3分野が該当。一方、最終需要では5分野が該当し、消費財等の部門でより強く競合している可能性を示唆
（注） ADBの国際産業連関表（実質ベース、2010年基準）をASEAN5の国際産業連関表へと加工し、中間投入・最終需要品それぞれにおける国産品・中国産品の金額を求めたもの
（出所） ADBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

22
 EUは中国製品の流入拡大を背景に、貿易措置を積極化
― 対中貿易措置は2023年以降に増加。化学品や鉄鋼など幅広い品目が対象。2024年には中国製EVへの追加関税を発

― 一方、直近ではドイツも保護主義路線へと徐々にシフト。背景には主力産業である自動車業界による路線変更
 ASEANも貿易保護措置を拡大する傾向。鉄鋼製品等へのアンチダンピング・セーフガードのほか、越境ECへも対応を拡大
― ただし、対中貿易措置の比率は目立って高まらず。外交関係への配慮から、中国以外の地域も含む形で規制を強化

「チャイナショック2.0」の光と影――安価な製品供給の恩恵と先進国の競争力喪失リスク
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」は、世界市場に低価格と安定供給という恩恵をもたらしている
― 中国の輸出価格が相対的に低下する一方、輸出数量はハイペースで拡大しており、輸出ドライブが鮮明に
― 輸出ドライブによる安価な中国製品の供給拡大が世界の財価格を押し下げ、インフレ圧力の緩和にも寄与
 他方で、中国の高い輸出競争力は輸入国の産業競争力の低下と対中依存度の上昇を招いていると考えられる

― 先進国が中国企業の技術革新力とコスト競争力に圧倒され、得意としてきた先端産業で競争力を喪失するリスク
― 関税などで対中輸入を抑制しても、供給網を通じた中国依存は容易に低下せず。経済安保上の脆弱性は残存
26

（出所） CPB、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 28

「チャイナショック2.0」への対応――国内製造業の強化で対抗しつつ、中国製品の輸入も継続
 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
― 先進国は、戦略分野での産業基盤の強化や対中デリスキングを図りつつ、非機微分野で貿易・投資活動を維持
― 新興国は、脱中国化を図る供給網の一部を取り込んで国内産業の育成を企図。過剰な中国製品の流入には警戒も
 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
― 過剰生産能力への批判を一蹴し輸出拡大。一方で、関税回避や規制対応のため、海外での現地生産も積極展開
27
「チャイナショック2.0」への対抗と協調

### Page 29

EUは産業競争力の回復に向け、政策ロードマップを策定。IAAにより域内調達も強化へ
 第二期フォンデアライエン体制下で、EUは「競争力回復」に向けた政策のロードマップ（競争力コンパス）を策定
― （１）米中とのイノベーション格差是正、（２）脱炭素化と競争力強化の両立、（３）過剰な域外依存の軽減と安全保障の強
化を目指す。加えて、市場統合の深化や規制緩和等を内容とする（４）分野横断的取り組みを実行する構え
 競争力コンパス枠組みのもと、欧州は原産地規則の強化により、EVや鉄鋼産業等の域内調達を強力に推進する方針
― 2026年3月以降、欧州委員会は域内調達の拡大に向け、IAA（産業加速法）の立法化に着手
― IAAの現行案では、EVの公共調達や補助金等の支援に対し、域内調達・組立の要件を導入予定
（注） 7要件：①実質的な内国民待遇、②経済安全保障、③競争条件＋ILO、④パリ協定＋炭素価格、⑤企業行動・人権、⑥低炭素需要政策、⑦迂回防止
（出所） 欧州委員会（2025）“A Competitiveness Compass for the EU(factsheet)”、ほか各種資料より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 31

政策の概要国
• 2026年9月、輸入電気自動車への物品税引上げを決定。バッテリーEVのうち国内生産がなく、国産部品も使
用していない車両には2027年から物品税を10％→35％に引き上げる計画
• 現地部品の使用など、一定の国内調達や今後の調達率引き上げが見込まれるものについては、税率引き下
げも検討
タイ
• 四輪電気自動車（BEV）について国産化率規定を設置。政府は2026年までに40％、2030年以降に80％と段
階的な基準値引き上げを目指す
• 完成車輸入優遇の利用企業に、2027年末までに輸入台数以上の国内生産を義務付け
インドネシア
• 2026年7月、EVの国内生産・部品製造を支援するEVインセンティブ戦略（EVIS）を導入。交付された納税証
明書を各種納税に充当することで、企業が税務負担を軽減できる仕組み
• 一定の投資・生産要件を満たす企業に対し、投資額の最大40％を支給
• 国内製造・組立車両の生産実績に応じ、工場出荷価格の最大12％相当を支給
フィリピン
• 国内組立EVや関連部品の税金を2027年末まで一部免除（EV本体：物品税・販売税、部品：輸入関税）

• 完成車輸入に車両価格、およびエンジン容量の下限を設定（2026年7月～）マレーシア
• 国内で製造・組立を行い、生産台数等の要件を満たす企業に、国内生産できない自動車部品の輸入関税を
免除。環境対応車も対象ベトナム
ASEANは、対中関係深化のメリットを維持すべく、自動車を中心に現地生産を促す
30
 ASEANは現地生産要件の設置などで、中国との貿易関係や中国企業の投資流入を維持しながら、輸入品との競合による
域内産業への圧力緩和を目指す方向
― 2025年以降、ASEAN5各国は、自動車を中心に現地生産の促進や国内規定を徐々に厳格化
― 輸入車を中心にEVの浸透が進む中、現地生産化の促進により、中国企業による域内投資や地場サプライヤー・雇用へ
の波及効果を得る狙い

### Page 32

おわりに：「チャイナショック2.0」とAI――製造業へのAI実装で一段と高まる中国の競争力
 中国は、AI技術で世界をリード。オープンウェイト戦略と国家主導のガバナンスで独自のAIエコシステムを構築しつつある
― 大規模言語モデルの性能は米国に肉薄。コストパフォーマンスで海外にも普及し、トークン消費量のシェアが急拡大
― 同時に、ヒューマノイドや自動運転といったフィジカルAI分野でも、技術開発と社会実装が急ピッチで進んでいる
 中国は今後、製造業へのAI実装によって生産性を向上、技術革新を加速させ、競争力を一段と高めるとみられる
― 中国政府は、経済社会の幅広い分野でAI導入を図る「AI＋（プラス）」政策を推進。「AI＋製造業」により研究開発設計、
生産、物流、サービスなど各バリューチェーンへの実装が進めば、「強すぎる供給力」がさらに強力に
31

### Page 33

「チャイナショック3.0」の可能性――中国がAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築
 AIと「強すぎる供給力」との融合が進めば、「チャイナショック2.0」が「3.0」へと発展する可能性も排除できず
 「チャイナショック3.0」は、中国がグローバルサウスにおいてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する現象
― 中国は独自の「AIエコシステム」の海外展開でAI経済圏を形成し、既存の技術秩序と産業・貿易構造を変容させる
― また、「世界AI協力機構（WAICO）」などを通じて国際協調を図り、AI経済に対応した標準・制度作りを主導する
― AIを軸とした「3.0」では、モノに留まらない形で中国の存在感が強まる可能性。世界にさらなる変化への対応を迫りうる

黃璽銘 hsiming.huang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

鴻海 (2317 TT) 
規模效益顯現

永豐觀點 
AI 伺服器為成長主軸，規模效益顯現，觀察政策長期對 AI 資料中心建置的影響。 
 投資評價與建議 
研究部維持鴻海買進建議：鴻海 3Q26 財報、4Q26 展望優於預期。2027 年維持
以 AI 伺服器為成長主軸，公司表示 2027 年 AI 伺服器產業需求仍大於供給，關鍵
零組件持續吃緊，惟仍需觀察期中選舉後，政策長期對大型企業 AI 資料中心建置
時程的變化。研究部認為鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同時體現獲利率效
益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用效率。研究部預估鴻海 2026 年/2027 年稅後
EPS 19.33 元/25.51 元。維持鴻海買進建議，目標價 356 元(14x2027FEPS)。

ESG 評析 
鴻海企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2025 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。
SinoPac Securities Investment Service licenses and applies the SASB Materiality Map® 
General Issue Categories and Disclosure Topics in our work.

### Page 2

2

鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產
品，以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯
(601138 SH)、富智康(2038 HK)及鴻騰精密(6088 HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研
發資源在電動車、數位醫療、機器人、AI、半導體、5G 等領域。主要客戶為 Apple、
HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、北美四大 CSP 等。3Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 54%、電腦終端 12%、元件及其他 5%。 
3Q26 財報優於預期：鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，營益率 3.83%，優於預估的
5.93%、3.77%。鴻海營收規模放大，營運槓桿效益顯現。業外收入貢獻 33 億元，
稅後淨利 775 億元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元，優於預估 5.10 元。3Q26 鴻海存貨金額
1.5 兆，季增 10%，年增 27%，存貨天數 45.7 天，季減 3 天，年減 4 天。存貨狀
況尚屬健康。 
4Q26 展望略優於預期：鴻海展望 4Q26 營運，AI 伺服器出貨量季增高雙位數(部
分專案採 Consignment 商業模式)，預期 ASIC 及 GPU 出貨續增，Vera Rubin AI
伺服器機櫃單價跳升，推估雲端網路營收季增約 15%。消費性產品 iPhone 維持
強勁出貨動能，PC 需求則持續疲弱季減。研究部略為上修 4Q26 營收預估季增
17%，稅後 EPS 上修至 5.99 元。 
AI 伺服器為鴻海營運增長動能 留意長期資料中心建置影響性：因應客戶 AI 建置
需求，鴻海持續支出投入產能，主要客戶為美系大型 CSP、AI 模型商及 Neocloud，
降低資金及庫存風險。並透過與客戶改成採用 Consignment 商業模式，提高營運
資金效率，並優化獲利率表現。研究部預期鴻海維持 Vera Rubin 的主要供應商角
色，整體市占率維持達 40%以上，同時鴻海 2027 年將增加美系 CSP AI ASIC 伺
服器機櫃供應，為 2027 年營運增長動能。公司表示 2027 年 AI 伺服器產業需求
仍大於供給，關鍵零組件持續吃緊，惟仍需觀察期中選舉後，政策長期對大型企業
AI 資料中心建置時程的變化。研究部認為鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同
時體現獲利率效益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用效率。 
綜合以上，研究部預估 2026 年營收 11.2 兆，年增 38%，毛利率 6.0%，營益率
3.7%，稅後淨利 2,710 億，年增 43%，稅後 EPS 19.33 元。2027 年營收 15.5 兆
元，年增 38%，毛利率 5.4%，營益率 3.4%，稅後淨利 3,562 億元，年增 31%，
稅後 EPS 25.51 元。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

Price target remains unchanged at NT$5,888
We continue to derive our base case value, which is also our price target, from a residual
income model. Our cost of equity of 10.4% (beta of 1.4, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and
risk-free rate of 2.0%) remains unchanged, as do our intermediate growth rate of  12.5%
and terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bear case value of NT$2,465 and bull case value of NT$6,880 are also unchanged.

MS Rating
▪ We continue to believe that AI ASICs will

outgrow AI GPUs in the coming years, and
Alchip is one of the few ASIC design service
providers that can deal with increasingly
complex designs (at 2nm) with good
production/design track records for large
leading edge chips.
▪ AWS's announcement of its 3nm
Trainium3 chip matches the timing of
Alchip's 3nm project win. The growing
number of use cases for Trainium (e.g.,
Apple's adoption) bodes well for Alchip's
revenue growth in 2026.
▪ We believe Alchip has strong potential to

win Trainium4.
▪ Our price target implies 26x our 2027e
EPS, which we ﬁnd undemanding vs. our
>100% revenue CAGR for 2025-28e.

NT$6,880.00
30x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to
proliferate, amid substantial end demand.

Alchip's leading-edge tape-out numbers
increase signiﬁcantly, with some high-proﬁle
project wins at 3nm or even 2nm. Alchip is
able to secure enough foundry capacity
(front-end wafers and back-end packaging)
and substrates for all of its leading-edge
turnkey production. Alchip gradually gains
market share within the design service
market, amid continued proliferation of
custom chip demand.
NT$5,888.00
26x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to

proliferate amid strong end demand,
resulting in steady growth in tape-outs and
volume. There might be a slowdown in
Alchip's Chinese customers' design activity
for leading-edge chips as a result of US
export controls; we believe this would be
largely offset by ASIC demand strength
from non-China regions.
NT$2,465.00
11x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects lack concrete
end demand, resulting in delayed tape-outs
and mass production. Alchip's leading-edge

project pipeline deteriorates, or some
customers turn directly to TSMC. AI GPU's
dominance in AI computing compresses the
market share gain potential for overall ASIC,
with customers starting to slow their
custom silicon developments - and therefore
ASIC procurement plans.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Investment Thesis: WNC 
WNC is a leading network communications solutions provider with technology across 
WiFi, Cellular, and Satellite. The company is the leading original design manufacturer 
(ODM) for major networking brands (e.g. Cisco, HP, etc.) and serves the leading US 
telecom operators (e.g. Verizon, AT&T, etc.). WNC is also the sole supplier of Starlink’s 
low earth orbits (LEO) satellites’ user terminal (power supply, antenna and router). We 
see WNC as an early beneficiary in the growing Space Economy, riding on Starlink’s 
subscribers growth, along with recovering traditional networking business driven by a 
low base in 2025, WiFi 7, 5G FWA (fixed wireless access) investments from US and 
Europe, and customers’ needs on diversifying suppliers, leading to a 14% NI CAGR in 
2026-28E. WNC is trading at 15x 2027E P/E, lower than the average of its main peers, 
which is attractive in our view. We are Buy rated on WNC. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation methodology: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for 
WNC, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on 
a target P/E multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived 
from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. 
Key downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Feedback:  Read across from Nan
Ya New Material's comments for
our coverage
Substrate CCL: Nan Ya New Material (not covered) has started shipping BT
substrate CCL in small volumes, primarily to domestic customers. We believe this is

driven by raw material supply constraints and has positive implications to non-
Japanese substrate CCL suppliers, including Elite Material, as customers diversify
away from incumbent suppliers.
Glass cloth: Nan Ya New Material expects the price trend of 7628-type glass cloth
to normalize from 2Q27 onwards, while thin/super-thin E glass cloth  remains very
under-supplied in CY27, which should provide support to thin E glass cloth pricing
through next year. The implication to Kingboard is therefore mixed.

Copper foil: Nan Ya New Material sees HTE copper foil declining while its demand
for advanced RTF and HVLP copper foils continues to surge, which bodes well for
Co-Tech, which has exited HTE copper foil business to focus on advanced RTF and
HVLP. The company also sources high-end HVLP from Co-Tech.
Customers: Nan Ya New Material mentioned major Chinese PCB vendors are all its
customers, including Shennan, WUS, Shengyi Elec, Founder, Kinwong, etc. For the

overseas market, it is also engaged with ISU, TTM and Unimicron, primarily for North
America-based customers.
Order change? Nan Ya New Material has not seen any server-related order cuts so
far; it noted the PCB/CCL  demand from the China compute market is growing
strongly. This also has positive implications to Chinese PCB/CCL suppliers Shennan
and Shengyi.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

率創歷史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700
萬美金，除了擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合
作關係。展望 2027 年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高
毛利的 Power 產品最為明顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表
現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026 年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年
EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷史 PE 區間為 10-35x，
考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔憂資料中心建置速
度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部屬之風險，
因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。 
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
貿聯-KY(3665)成立於 1996 年，為全球連接線大廠，總部位於美
國矽谷，生產據點則廣泛分布在全球 37 地。產品應用包含數據通
訊、工廠自動化、半導體設備、醫療、車用等，2025 年工業佔 35%、
通訊 42%、車用 11%、電器 11%、其他 1%。銷售主要為美國 20%、
中國 20%、德國 16%、馬來西亞 7%、台灣 3%、其他 34%。

Interplex 正式併表，產品組合有望再度優化 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.5 億元，M+26.9%，Y+80.0%，自 9 月起完成
對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達
6700 萬美金，約 20 億台幣，由於 Interplex 既有客戶與貿聯本身
有所重疊，然而產品項目卻有所不同，預計在透過集團整合能力下
將進一步推動公司在客戶群內滲透率的提升。此外貿聯本身新規格
的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台
興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個
百分點；2)高價原料成本衝擊：白銀在 2026 上半年曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史
天價，公司 8~9 月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料，對 8~9 月的單月毛利率造成短期壓抑。 
圖 1：松川精密應用別營收比重

資料來源：松川，國票投顧 
最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
展望 4Q26，10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符合
預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與原先預估差異不
大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，目前回落至 50 美元以下，原
料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影響性已降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情
況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至 29.11%，預估稅後淨利 2.65 億元，QoQ+17.58%，
YoY+139.24%，EPS 3.33 元。

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 267: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, +28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

A series of macro shocks, including
higher energy prices, rising Treasury
yields and shifting Federal Reserve
expectations, have tested markets,
but resilient economic growth, strong
corporate earnings and ongoing
artificial intelligence-related investment

continue to support a constructive
outlook for Equities and risk assets.
We remain overweight Equities, favor
U.S. stocks, see potential opportunities
in broader market participation and
select cyclical sectors, and view higher
bond yields as attractive to add income
and extend duration.

MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Mixed economic data, a surprise budget golden rule proposal, and

5.4% target, and will barely narrow in 2027.

Document 360: Is the economy ‘plucked’_ (2026-11-25)

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

Figure 1: Unemployment rate in Australia
*Long-run NAIRU estimate from Ballantyne and Cusbert (2025).
1. Introduction
The unemployment rate in the data has strongly nonlinear dynamic (Figure 1). It spikes sharply in
occasional downturns, then declines slowly but persistently in recoveries.1 It is right-skewed in both
levels (i.e. asymmetric ‘deepness’) and in changes (i.e. asymmetric ‘steepness’) (Sichel 1993). Gross
flows between unemployment and employment display similar nonlinearities. Standard search-and-

matching models of the labour market struggle to generate these nonlinearities in unemployment
and labour-market flows (Ferraro 2023).
If this asymmetric pattern stems from frictions within the labour market, what implication do those
frictions have for the relationship between inflation and unemployment? Research using nonlinear
structural models to explore the asymmetric dynamics in the labour market tend to abstract from price
frictions and inflation dynamics (e.g. Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson 2025; Ferraro 2018; Petrosky-

Nadeau, Zhang and Kuehn 2018), while in the New Keynesian literature, the usual approach of
linearising equilibrium conditions excludes these nonlinear dynamics by assumption. The 2020s
inflation surge has reignited research into the nonlinearity of the Phillips curve, and the extent to which
this nonlinearity stems from frictions within the labour market (e.g. Benigno and Eggertsson 2023).
But this literature tends not to model the labour market in as much detail.

In this paper, we explore the extent to which a detailed search-and-matching model of the labour
market with pricing frictions can match the nonlinear dynamics in the labour market. We then use
the model to examine the implications of these nonlinearities for the Phillips curve relationship and
the non-accelerating rate of unemployment (NAIRU).
1 Friedman (1964) uses the ‘plucking’ description for the asymmetric way in which output seems to be occasionally ‘plucked’

below trend, before recovering back to trend.

### Page 5

2
We start with a general-equilibrium search-and-matching model similar to Bloesch, Lee and
Weber (2024), in which firms post wages and vacancies and set prices (subject to nominal rigidities
and recruiting costs), and workers search (while unemployed and on the job) and decide whether to
accept and quit jobs. Policy steers aggregate demand to balance inflation and unemployment targets.
We extend this model to include endogenous layoffs, downward nominal wage rigidity, and a ‘slippery’

job ladder (drawing on Dupraz et al (2025)).2 We find that these features - together with convex
recruiting costs - are collectively key to achieving both the asymmetric spikes in unemployment when
shocks occur and the slow speed in the subsequent recovery. The model is sufficiently detailed to
broadly match the dynamics of unemployment and gross flows, but tractable enough that we can
obtain the global nonlinear solution with rational expectations and aggregate uncertainty. This is

important because agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty is a significant determinant
of outcomes, especially the long-run NAIRU.
We show that the model can generate substantial asymmetry in both ‘deepness’ and ‘steepness’
(comparable to or greater than in the data). The response of unemployment, job-finding, and
separations after a stylised negative demand shock broadly matches the response of these variables in
downturns over the past 50 years. Aggregate demand shocks have an especially asymmetric effect,

because the effect on inflation amplifies the effect of the downward nominal wage rigidity. When
supply shocks occur, the inflation response somewhat eases the downward nominal wage constraint.
The model endogenously generates a Phillips curve relationship that is strongly nonlinear and state-
dependent. Conditional on some initial state of the economy, the Phillips curve is much steeper for
low rates of unemployment. When the initial unemployment rate is higher, the Phillips curve shifts to

the right, and when the initial level of real wages is higher, it shifts upwards.
We can also use the model to simulate a ‘short-run NAIRU’, which is the unemployment rate consistent
with eliminating inflationary pressure from the labour market at some cyclical horizon (e.g. 1-2 years).
This short-run NAIRU varies asymmetrically with the state of the economy. It tends to rise when
unemployment is high, but also when unemployment has fallen rapidly (because real wages increase).

Finally, we can use the ‘stochastic steady state’ of the model to explore the determinants of the long-
run NAIRU, i.e. the unemployment rate consistent with stable inflation in the absence of shocks (but
where agents still expect that shocks may occur in the future). We find that when firms account for
the interaction between aggregate risk and asymmetric wage adjustment costs in their wage-setting
decisions, it generates a trade-off between unemployment variance and steady-state unemployment.

For example, if policy places greater weight on reducing the gap between unemployment and the
long-run NAIRU (instead of the gap between inflation and target), it tends to increase the long-run
NAIRU. This is because by mitigating fluctuations in labour demand, policy reduces the risk of hitting
downward nominal wage rigidities. Firms are then more willing to set higher real wages in steady
state, which reduces the optimal amount of employment in steady state.

The remainder of this introduction discusses the literature. Section 2 outlines the model. Section 3
evaluates the extent to which it can match the nonlinear dynamics in labour-market data. Section 4
considers the implications for the Phillips curve and NAIRU.
2 By ‘slippery’ job ladder, we mean that workers recently hired out of unemployed are much more likely to be laid off.

### Page 6

3
Literature review. The cyclical asymmetries in unemployment are established empirically by e.g.
Sichel (1993), McKay and Reis (2008), and Dupraz et al (2025). Ferraro (2023) analyses the ability of
the baseline Diamond-Mortensen-Pissarides (DMP) model to produce these asymmetries, and finds
that it cannot generate the ‘steepness’ asymmetry (i.e. right-skewness in unemployment changes).
Petrosky-Nadeau et al (2018) finds that the DMP model can generate the ‘deepness’ asymmetry given

large enough shocks.
Dupraz et al (2025) and Ferraro (2018) propose search-and-matching models that can generate
sharp spikes and slow declines in unemployment. We take several features from Dupraz et al (2025),
including downward nominal wage rigidity and a ‘slippery’ job ladder. Pizzinelli, Theodoridis and
Zanetti (2020) explore the state-dependence of unemployment fluctuations in a model with
endogenous layoffs and on-the-job search.3 Adjemian, Karamé and Langot (2021) estimate a

nonlinear model with endogenous separations, downward wage rigidity, worker heterogeneity and
segmented markets, and show that it can generate similar asymmetries in unemployment, hiring,
and separations to the data.
Our definition of a ‘short-run NAIRU’ is similar to the concept discussed by Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.), who
argue that the natural rate of unemployment may vary cyclically and track the actual unemployment
rate during recoveries. Hall and Kudlyak (2022) discusses the ability of DMP-style models to generate

the slow persistent recovery in unemployment that occurs after recessions. Fujita and Ramey (2007)
show that adding vacancy adjustment costs to a DMP model can produce a more sluggish response
for vacancies and hiring after a downturn, slowing down the recovery in unemployment. Mercan,
Schoefer and Sedláček (2024) suggest a congestion mechanism, whereby new hires and incumbent
workers are imperfect substitutes in production, which also propagates unemployment after a shock.

This literature above generally abstracts from price rigidities and inflation dynamics. There is a
substantial literature incorporating labour-market frictions into New Keynesian models (see e.g.
Blanchard and Galí 2010). Recent examples include Moscarini and Postel-Vinay (2023), Faccini and
Melosi (2025), and Bloesch et al (2024). These papers focus on the role of on-the-job search and wage
competition between firms in inflation dynamics. We adopt a similar model for wage setting, drawing

on the tractable formulation in Bloesch et al (2024). But these papers use linear approximations.
The literature on downward nominal wage rigidity explores the extent to which it generates a
nonlinear Phillips curve and asymmetric cyclical dynamics (see e.g. Kim and Ruge-Murcia 2009;
Abbritti and Fahr 2013; Daly and Hobijn 2014; Benigno and Antonio Ricci 2011; Schmitt-Grohé
and Uribe 2022; Benigno and Eggertsson 2023; Benigno and Eggertsson 2024). Lepetit (2020)

also constructs a New Keynesian model with asymmetric unemployment dynamics, although the
asymmetry stems from the convex relationship between the job-finding rate and unemployment.
This literature does not model the labour market in as much detail (e.g. endogenous layoffs, on-the-
job search, job ladder) and does not seek to match the ‘plucking’ property in unemployment to the
same extent.
3 Fujita and Ramey (2012) also analyse versions of the DMP model with endogenous separations and on-the-job search,

but their focus is not on asymmetries in unemployment rate dynamics

### Page 7

4
2. Model
The model consists of firms, workers, and a policy rule. We start by outlining the firms’ problem
and optimal choices. Firms’ optimal decision-making drives most of the model’s mechanics. We then
discuss workers and the policy regime, which are modelled more simply. 5 contains a full list of
equilibrium conditions, as well as details of the calibration and solution method.
2.1 Firms
Firms make decisions around hiring, separations, and price- and wage-setting.

Hiring: Firms choose how many vacancies to post, V i t . These vacancies generate Mi t = qtV i t matches,
where qt is determined by a matching function and depends on aggregate matching efficiency and
labour market tightness. A proportion 1 − ϕ E t of these matches are unemployed searchers (where ϕ E t
is the share of unemployed searchers in total searchers, weighted by search intensity).
Firms must screen matches. Then the individual productivities of employed matches are revealed.

Firms make offers to those with productivities above some threshold xi t , which is 1−F i t of them (where
F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual productivity distribution). For unemployed matches,
firms have no information about their individual productivity and make offers to all of them. This
feature proxies for the idea that firms may be more uncertain about the capabilities of unemployed
applicants.
Matches with offers choose whether to accept them; Ace t (W i t ) and employed matches and Acu t (W i t )

of unemployed matches do so, where W i t is the real wage paid by the firm. These job-acceptance
probabilities are characterised in the description of workers below.
The number of new hires is given by
Hi t = ϕ E t Mi t (1 − F i t )Ace t (W i t ) + (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ).
Define Hi,u t = (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ) as the number of hires from unemployment.

Separations: There are two types of separation: quits and layoffs. Each period, Qt (W i t ) of firms’
existing employees quit (either to unemployment or to another job). Just as firms’ hiring rate is

increasing in the real wage they offer (via the job-acceptance probabilities Ace t (W i t ) and Acu t (W i t )),
their quits rate is decreasing in the real wage by the firm. This function is characterised in the section
below on workers.
Workers’ probability of being laid off depends on whether they are insecurely or securely attached.
Insecurely attached employees are all those who have just been hired out of unemployed in
the previous period. Before aggregate shocks are revealed, some portion of new hires from

### Page 8

5
unemployment, δ u, (i.e. who were hired the previous period), draw a new productivity from
distribution G. Those whose productivities are less than some threshold xi,m t−1 are laid off, which
is Gi t−1 of them (where Gi t = G(xi,m t−1) and G is the CDF of the distribution).4 All insecurely attached
employees who are not laid off then because securely attached (together with all those who have
been employed for more than one period).
After aggregate shocks are revealed and quitting decisions made, δ of securely attached employees

draw a new individual productivity. Firms then decide to lay off those whose productivities are less
than some threshold xi t , which is F i t of them (where F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual
productivity distribution). The layoff probability for securely attached employees will be lower than
for insecurely attached employees both because δ < δ u and the distribution F has less mass at low
values than G.

2.2 Workers
All workers who are unemployed when shocks are revealed search for jobs. A share λ EE of employed
workers also search. The total number of searchers is then λ EE ˜Nt−1 + 1 − ˜Nt−1, and the effective
share of employed searchers is
ϕ E t = λ EE ˜Nt−1
λ EE ˜Nt−1 + 1 − ˜Nt−1 .
Those who enter unemployment after shocks are revealed, either because they are laid off or because

they quit to unemployment, do not search in that period.6
A share λ EU of employed workers consider quitting to unemployment.
6 This means that even if hiring costs went to zero, unemployment would remain above zero. If the newly unemployed
were allowed to search, this would raise the average number of searchers, especially in periods where layoffs are high.
Workers hired last period who are laid off before shocks are revealed can search. This is to avoid Hu t−1 or Nt−1 being a

state variable.

Workers’ only role in the model is to determine the labour supply schedule faced by firms. They do so
by determining the hiring and quits elasticities ε He t , ε Hu t and ε Q t via the functions Ace t (Wt ), Acu t (Wt )
and Q(Wt ).
